If the N-terminal ~46 residues are extracellular and the C-terminal portion has some extracellular/cytoplasmic content, then the TM region spans roughly positions 47 to ~430.

## 6,622. judge:sequence-mcq-expansion-v1-00094

**Question type:** catalytic_activity

<table>
<thead><tr><th>Original question</th><th>Skill-informed rewrite</th></tr></thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which reaction best describes the catalytic activity of this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> 3-Phosphoglycerate + ATP → 1,3-bisphosphoglycerate + ADP<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Shikimate + ATP → 3-phosphoshikimate + ADP + H⁺<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Ribulose-5-phosphate + ATP → ribulose-1,5-bisphosphate + ADP<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Glucose + ATP → glucose-6-phosphate + ADP + H⁺<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br>Let me analyze this protein sequence systematically:<br><br>**Sequence length**: The protein is approximately 245 amino acids long.<br><br>**Key motif identification**: The sequence contains &quot;GMMGCGKTTVGR&quot; around position 110-125, which is a classic Walker A motif (G-x-x-G-x-G-K-T/S), indicating this is an ATP-binding kinase.<br><br>**Evaluating each option**:<br><br>- **Option D (Hexokinase)**: Hexokinases are typically 400-500+ amino acids long. This protein is too short to be a hexokinase. ❌<br><br>- **Option A (Phosphoglycerate kinase)**: PGK proteins are typically 400+ amino acids.
